Equivariant Partially Wrapped Fukaya Categories on Liouville Sectors
Dongwook Choa, Jiawei Hu, Siu-Cheong Lau, Yan-Lung Leon Li

Abstract
. We develop an equivariant Lagrangian Floer theory for Liouville sectors that have symmetry of a Lie group G G . Moreover, for Liouville manifolds with G G -symmetry, we develop a correspondence theory to relate the equivariant Lagrangian Floer cohomology upstairs and Lagrangian Floer cohomology of its quotient. Furthermore, we study the symplectic quotient in the presence of nodal type singularities and prove that the equivariant correspondence gives an isomorphism on cohomologies which was conjectured by Lekili-Segal.



1.
Introduction

We introduce an equivariant wrapped Fukaya category for Liouville manifolds (or sectors) equipped with a Liouville action by a compact Lie group
G
. This construction is carried out through a variant of the Borel construction in which the homotopy quotient is modeled by approximations of a sequence of Liouville manifolds (sectors). Within this framework, we develop a Lagrangian correspondence theory which, in the case of a free action, generates a fully faithful
A_{\infty}
-functor from a full subcategory of the equivariant wrapped Fukaya category to the ordinary wrapped Fukaya category of the exact symplectic quotient.

Let
X
be a symplectic manifold whose mirror is denoted by
X^{\vee}
. Teleman conjectured that a Hamiltonian
G
action on
X
is mirror to a holomorphic fibration

F:X^{\vee}\to G^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}/\mathrm{Ad}

where
G^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}
is the complexified Langlands dual group, and
G^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}/\mathrm{Ad}
denotes the space of conjugacy classes, such that
the mirror of a symplectic quotient
X/\!\!/_{\xi}\,G
at a regular moment-map level
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{*}
is given by a fiber
X^{\vee}_{\phi(\xi)}=F^{-1}\{\phi(\xi)\}
for some
\phi(\xi)\in G^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}/\mathrm{Ad}
. Equivariant quantum cohomology in this setup was studied in
[
56
,
25
,
26
]
and equivariant open-string mirror symmetry was studied in
[
33
,
37
]
.

In this paper, we study the equivariant partially wrapped Fukaya category of a Liouville sector with a Hamiltonian
G
-symmetry. The theory of partially wrapped Fukaya categories for Liouville sectors developed by
[
19
,
20
]
enjoys nice functorial properties and provides a powerful tool of sectorial decompositions to study wrapped Fukaya categories.
One important motivation is the Fukaya-Seidel category of a Landau-Ginzburg model
(Y,W)
, which can be understood as the partially wrapped theory for the corresponding Liouville sector obtained by taking a page of superpotential as Liouville hypersurface. Landau-Ginzburg models appear as the mirrors of Fano or general-type varieties and their Fukaya-Seidel categories reflect the derived categories of coherent sheaves of these varieties.

For simplicity, let us consider
G=\mathbb{S}^{1}
(while the general theory is developed for a general compact Lie group
G
). By homological mirror symmetry
[
35
]
, the conjecture can be reformulated as follows.

Conjecture 1.1
.

Let
X
be a Liouville sector equipped with a Hamiltonian
\mathbb{S}^{1}
-action. Suppose that its symplectic quotient
\underline{X}
at the zero moment level is smooth and is itself a Liouville sector. Then there exists a
\mathbb{C}[s]
-module structure on the Hochschild cohomology
HH^{*}(\mathcal{W}(X))
such that
HH^{*}(\mathcal{W}(\underline{X}))
is isomorphic to the quotient of the Hochschild cohomology of the partially wrapped Fukaya category of
\mathbb{S}^{1}
-invariant objects in
X
by the ideal generated by
(s-1)
.

We develop the theory of equivariant wrapped Fukaya category and correspondence to tackle this conjecture. The theory of Lagrangian correspondence was developed by
[
44
,
18
]
and
[
22
,
23
]
in the wrapped setting. In
[
37
]
, we constructed equivariant Lagrangian correspondence for symplectic quotients in the compact setup using the machinery of
[
18
]
where Kuranishi perturbations were essential. In the current exact setting, we can use geometric Hamiltonian perturbations in place of Kuranishi perturbation techniques. Moreover, the Lagrangian correspondence is tautologically unobstructed since it does not bound any non-constant holomorphic disk, and we can construct the cyclic element without using boundary deformation of Lagrangian submanifolds.

1.1.
Equivariant wrapped Fukaya category via Borel construction

The first step of our construction is to define an
equivariant wrapped Fukaya category

\mathcal{W}_{G}(X)
associated to a Liouville manifold (or sector)
X
, which carries an action of a compact Lie group
G
preserving the Liouville form. Throughout, we always assume that
X
is of
finite type
(i.e. with compact skeleton). The objects of
\mathcal{W}_{G}(X)
are embedded, exact,
G
-invariant Lagrangian submanifolds that are conical near infinity. The morphism complexes are defined as homotopy colimits of the corresponding equivariant Floer complexes, taken along the flow of a
G
-invariant wrapping Hamiltonian.

Classically,
G
-equivariant cohomology may be viewed as a functor
H_{G}^{\bullet}(-)
on the category of
G
-spaces. One way to construct it is via the
Borel construction
:

\displaystyle H_{G}^{\bullet}(X)\coloneqq H^{\bullet}(X_{G}),\quad X_{G}\coloneqq X\times_{G}EG,

where
EG
is a contractible space on which
G
acts freely. The associated quotient
X_{G}
plays a double role:

(1)

a locally trivial fibration
X\to X_{G}\to BG
;

(2)

a (typically non-locally trivial) fibration

B\operatorname{Stab}(x)\to X_{G}\to[x]\in X/G,

where
B\operatorname{Stab}(x)
is the classifying space of the stabilizer of
x
.

When the
G
-action is free, these identifications immediately yield
H_{G}^{\bullet}(X)\cong H^{\bullet}(X/G).

There is a substantial body of work extending this perspective to Floer theory in various settings; see Section
1.4
for a brief summary.
A complication in our setting is that we must work with
families of Floer equations
parametrized by the infinite-dimensional Hilbert manifold
BG
.
In infinite dimensions, many familiar tools from Morse and Floer theory break down—one reason is that closed balls in Hilbert space are never compact. To avoid these analytic difficulties, we adopt a
finite-dimensional approximation
approach and prove that Floer theory behaves compatibly along these approximations.

A more subtle issue arises when considering families of Floer equations parametrized by Morse trajectories on
BG
. For a general choice of model
EG\to BG
and an arbitrary connection on this principal bundle, fiberwise exact Lagrangians need not be preserved under boundary parallel transport.

To address this, we construct
X_{G}
as the colimit of a sequence of Liouville manifolds (or sectors)

\displaystyle X(1)\hookrightarrow X(2)\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow X(N)\hookrightarrow\cdots,

each equipped with the structure of a Liouville fibration.
This setup allows us to reduce the equivariant theory to Floer theory in a symplectic (Liouville) fibration, a setting that has already been extensively studied—most notably in the context of Lefschetz fibrations. Within such framework, we construct the equivariant wrapped Fukaya category
\mathcal{W}_{G}(X)
.

A fundamental structural result for (non-wrapped) equivariant Floer cohomology is that it satisfies an analogue of the Piunikhin-Salamon-Schwarz (PSS) isomorphism
[
55
]
.

Theorem 1.2

(Theorem
4.20
)
.

For an embedded
G
-invariant exact cylindrical Lagrangian, there is an isomorphism

\displaystyle H_{G}^{\bullet}(L;\mathbb{K})\cong HF_{G}^{\bullet}(L,L)

which we call the
G
-equivariant PSS isomorphism.

In particular, when the
G
-action on
L
is free, the isomorphism
H_{G}^{\bullet}(L)\cong H^{\bullet}(L/G)
combined with the usual PSS isomorphism implies that the equivariant Floer cohomology reduces to ordinary Floer cohomology on the quotient

HF_{G}^{\bullet}(L,L)\cong HF^{\bullet}(L/G,L/G).

To extend this correspondence to pairs of cylindrical Lagrangians with wrapping, and to make the construction functorial, we will employ the technique of Lagrangian correspondences in the wrapped setting.

1.2.
Equivariant Lagrangian correspondence

Assume that the
G
-action on the zero level set of the moment map is free, so that the symplectic quotient
X/\!\!/_{0}\,G\coloneqq\mu^{-1}(0)/G
is smooth.
1
1

1

Throughout the paper, we will simply write
X/\!\!/G
for
X/\!\!/_{0}\,G
, since we always work at the zero moment level.
Since the homotopy quotient
X_{G}
is modeled as the colimit of a sequence of Liouville manifolds, using finite-dimensional approximations, we study the Lagrangian correspondence

\displaystyle L_{G}^{\pi}\subset X_{G}^{-}\times X/\!\!/G.

This will produce an
A_{\infty}
-functor relating the equivariant wrapped Fukaya category
\mathcal{W}_{G}(X)
and the (ordinary) wrapped Fukaya category
\mathcal{W}(X/\!\!/G)
.

Our approach follows the strategy of Fukaya
[
18
]
, which constructs an
A_{\infty}
-bimodule equipped with a distinguished
cyclic element
. The key ingredient is an extension to the Liouville setting of a similar construction previously developed in the compact case
[
37
]
. This produces the following
A_{\infty}
-functor.

Theorem 1.3

(Theorem
6.15
)
.

Let
X
be a Liouville manifold equipped with a free action of a compact Lie group
G
preserving the Liouville form. Let
\mathcal{W}_{G}(X)_{0}
denote the full subcategory of
\mathcal{W}_{G}(X)
generated by
G
-invariant Lagrangians contained in
\mu^{-1}(0)
. Then there exists a fully faithful
A_{\infty}
-functor

\displaystyle\Psi_{\downarrow}:\mathcal{W}_{G}(X)_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{W}(X/\!\!/G),

which sends a
G
-invariant exact Lagrangian
L\subset X
to its quotient
L/G
. Therefore,
\mathcal{W}(X/\!\!/G)
is quasi-equivalent to the full subcategory
\mathcal{W}_{G}(X)_{0}
.

The same result extends to Liouville sectors, provided that the Floer data are chosen carefully so that the target space has bounded geometry. We do not address these technicalities here, and refer the reader to
[
19
,
20
]
for further details.

For a path of Hamiltonian diffeomorphisms on a symplectic manifold
X
, one has the open Seidel morphism on Lagrangian Floer cohomologies defined by
[
29
,
28
,
12
]
.
In the case of a Hamiltonian
\mathbb{S}^{1}
-action, the closed-open image
CO(S)
of the Seidel element
[
60
]
gives an element
s\in HH^{*}(\mathcal{W}(X))
, which is obtained by counting pseudoholomorphic polygons with an interior insertion by the Seidel element. For invariant Lagrangians,
CO(S)
is closely related to the equivariant disk potential
[
33
]
, which takes a very simple form for a single exact Lagrangian submanifold since it does not bound any non-constant pseudoholomorphic disk. This will be useful to construct the conjectural isomorphism between the quotient of
s-1
and
HH^{*}(\mathcal{W}(\underline{X}))
in Conjecture
1.1
.

1.3.
Singular symplectic quotients

However, in the exact setup where the moment correspondence
L^{\pi}
is required to be exact, the action on
L^{\pi}
is not free even in simple examples.

Example 1.4
.

Let us consider the Hamiltonian
\mathbb{S}^{1}
-action with weights
(1,-1)
on
\mathbb{C}^{2}
. The action is free on all moment levels
\xi\not=0
, and it has the fixed point
0
on the exact level
\xi=0
. The quotient
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\!\!/_{0}\,\mathbb{S}^{1}
has a singular symplectic structure.

On non-exact levels
\xi\not=0
, the equivariant Lagrangian correspondence
L^{\pi}_{\mathbb{S}^{1}}
is obstructed due to contributions of holomorphic disks contained in the
x
- and
y
-axes of
\mathbb{C}^{2}
. This obstruction leads to a discrepancy between the disk potential
W_{\mathbb{C}^{2}}=T^{A}(z+T^{\xi}w)
for standard Lagrangian tori in
\mathbb{C}^{2}
, when restricted to the Teleman’s fiber
zw^{-1}=1
, and the disk potential
W_{\mathbb{C}}=T^{A}z
of the quotient. To eliminate this obstruction, it is necessary to introduce a bulk deformation of
\mathbb{C}
by
\mathbf{b}=\log(1+T^{\xi})[\mathrm{pt}]
so that both potentials become
T^{A}(1+T^{\xi})z
. See
[
37
, Example 1.4]
for a closely related example. If we use a non-trivial spin structure for
L^{\pi}
, the sign is changed and we obtain the potential
T^{A}(1-T^{\xi})z
(with
\mathbf{b}=\log(1-T^{\xi})[\mathrm{pt}]
). With this bulk deformation
\mathbf{b}
, the
\mathbb{S}^{1}
-equivariant Fukaya category of
\mathbb{C}^{2}
at the moment level
\xi
corresponds to the Fukaya category of
(\mathbb{C},\mathbf{b})
.

As
\xi\to 0
, the required bulk deformation blows up to
-\infty
. This leads to a pinching of
\mathbb{C}
at the origin, where
\mathbb{C}^{*}
has zero disk potential. In such a situation, we expect that the equivariant Lagrangian correspondence at the limiting exact level
\xi\to 0
would give a quasi-equivalence between the
\mathbb{S}^{1}
-equivariant wrapped category of
\mathbb{C}^{2}
with the wrapped category of
\mathbb{C}^{*}
. In the more general case of
X
being the total space of conic fibrations, this was conjectured by Lekili-Segal
[
40
]
.

Under symplectic reduction of a symplectic manifold
X
at a singular value of the moment map, the quotient
\underline{X}
is in general a singular symplectic space. One may remove the singular locus to obtain a smooth open symplectic manifold
\underline{X}^{\circ}
. In certain cases, one can equip it with a suitable Liouville structure, and study its wrapped Fukaya category.

In a series of examples, Lekili-Segal
[
40
]
observed an intersecting phenomenon that the equivariant Lagrangian Floer cohomology of a Lagrangian
L\subset X
is isomorphic to the wrapped Floer cohomology of the corresponding Lagrangian
\underline{L}^{\circ}\subset\underline{X}^{\circ}
. They conjectured that this correspondence holds in general. It is not known whether a Lagrangian in
\underline{X}^{\circ}
admits a compactification to a smooth Lagrangian in
X
in full generality. Thus, we impose a suitable local model for such compactifications. Under this assumption, and using the machinery developed in this paper, we prove the following result.

Theorem 1.5

(see Theorem
7.8
for the precise statement)
.

Let
X
be a symplectic manifold with a Hamiltonian
\mathbb{S}^{1}
-action, and let
\mu:X\to\mathbb{R}
denote the moment map. Consider the symplectic quotient
\underline{X}=\mu^{-1}(0)/\mathbb{S}^{1}
and let
\underline{X}^{\circ}
be the complement of the singular locus of
\mu^{-1}(0)
. Assume that the isotropy group of each point in
\mu^{-1}(0)
is either trivial or the whole
\mathbb{S}^{1}
, and that only nodal singularities occur. Then, for invariant Lagrangians in
\mu^{-1}(0)\subset X
which are of disk type near the singularities, the equivariant Floer cohomology is isomorphic to the wrapped Floer cohomology of the quotient in
\underline{X}^{\circ}
.

1.4.
Relevant works in literature

Various equivariant extensions of Lagrangian Floer theory have been studied in the literature since the work of Khovanov and Seidel
[
31
]
. We refer to
[
37
, Section 1]
and the references therein for a summary, and to
[
32
]
for another formulation of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant wrapped Floer theory. In this paper, we focus on the equivariant Floer theory for a Lie group
G
.

In a range of classes of geometries, substantial evidence has been found linking equivariant Floer theory to singular symplectic quotients; see, for instance
[
37
,
39
,
46
,
8
,
67
]
. Lekili and Segal formulate several conjectures in this direction. The conjectures most relevant to our work are Conjecture A, D and E in
[
40
]
.

In this paper, we consider a general Liouville manifold
X
with a Liouville
\mathbb{S}^{1}
-action whose quotient
X/\!\!/\,\mathbb{S}^{1}
has at worst nodal type singularities. We establish an isomorphism between the equivariant Floer cohomology of
X
and wrapped Floer cohomology of the quotient
X/\!\!/\,\mathbb{S}^{1}
.

For an additive hypertoric variety
X
, Lee, Li, Liu and Mak
[
39
]
used sectorial descent to compute the partially wrapped Fukaya category of its hyperplane arrangement in terms of its equivariant convolution algebra, which is (conjecturally) related to its equivariant partially wrapped Fukaya category;
for a multiplicative hypertoric variety
X
, using microlocal sheaf theory, McBreen, Shende and Zhou
[
46
]
proved a “Fukaya category commutes with reduction” theorem in terms of module action on categories.

The second author would like to thank Denis Auroux and Daniel Pomerleano for helpful discussions. The third and fourth authors express their gratitude to Naichung Conan Leung for great discussions in The Chinese University of Hong Kong and sharing his insights on 3D mirror symmetry and Teleman’s conjecture.

Notations and conventions

For the convenience of the reader, we summarize some notations and conventions used throughout this paper. They are ordered according to their first appearance in the main text.

•

Lie groups and actions.
All the Lie groups are assumed to be compact and connected. When a Lie group
G
acts on a manifold
X
, we denote the action by an element
g
on a point
x\in X
by
\tau_{g}(x)
, or
g\cdot x
for short. The vector field on
X
induced by a Lie algebra element
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}
is denoted by
X_{\xi}
.

•

Hamiltonian vector fields.
The sign convention for a Hamiltonian vector field is

\omega(X_{H},\cdot)=-dH.

•

Morse and Floer theory conventions.
We adopt the “back-propagation” convention. For a Riemann disk, the negative puncture corresponds to the output of the
\mu^{k}
operations, while the positive punctures correspond to the inputs.
The similar convention applies to Floer strips, as well as to Morse trajectories and Morse trees.

•

Wrapped Floer complexes.
In contrast to
[
19
]
, where the wrapped Floer complex is obtained as the colimit

\displaystyle CF^{\bullet}(L^{0},K)\rightarrow CF^{\bullet}(L^{1},K)\rightarrow\cdots,

with respect to the inverse system
\cdots\rightarrow L^{1}\rightarrow L^{0}
, our wrapped Floer complex is defined as the colimit

\displaystyle CF^{\bullet}(L,K^{0})\rightarrow CF^{\bullet}(L,K^{1})\rightarrow\cdots

with respect to the direct system
K^{0}\rightarrow K^{1}\rightarrow\cdots
. The reason for this choice is that, in our situation, it is often more convenient to consider pseudo-holomorphic strips
u
bounded by
L_{0}
and
L_{1}
with a Hamiltonian perturbation term. Such a strip corresponds bijectively to an honest holomorphic strip by setting:
\tilde{u}\coloneqq(\phi^{t}_{H})^{-1}(u)
, with boundary conditions
L_{0}
and
(\phi^{1}_{H})^{-1}(L_{1})
.

•

Categories and
A_{\infty}
-categories.
Unless stated otherwise, all categories are assumed to be small. Our
A_{\infty}
-composition follows the same “forward-composition” convention as in
[
19
,
20
]
.
The
A_{\infty}
-operations take the form

\displaystyle\mu^{d}:\mathcal{C}(X_{0},X_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{C}(X_{d-1},X_{d})\rightarrow\mathcal{C}(X_{0},X_{d})[2-d].

•

A_{\infty}
-modules.
The conventions for
A_{\infty}
left modules, right modules, and bimodules are defined accordingly with respect to the
A_{\infty}
-operations specified above; see
[
19
]
for details. For an
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{C}
, we denote by
\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathcal{C}}
the dg-category of
left

\mathcal{C}
-modules, and by
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathcal{C}}
the dg-category of
right

\mathcal{C}
-modules.

2.
Group actions on Liouville manifolds or sectors

2.1.
Liouville manifolds

References for Liouville manifolds and the relations with contact manifolds include
[
15
,
63
,
19
]
. To fix notation and convention, we briefly recall several standard definitions.

A vector filed
Z
on a symplectic manifold
(X,\omega)
of dimension
2n
is called
Liouville
if
\mathcal{L}_{Z}\omega=\omega
. By Cartan’s formula, this is equivalent to
\omega=d\lambda
where
\lambda\coloneqq\iota_{Z}\omega
is the associated
Liouville form
. The flow
\psi_{Z}^{s}
of
Z
exponentially expands both
\lambda
and
\omega
, i.e.

\displaystyle(\psi_{Z}^{s})^{*}\lambda=e^{s}\lambda,\quad(\psi_{Z}^{s})^{*}\omega=e^{s}\omega.

A (co-oriented)
contact structure
on a manifold
Y
of dimension
2n-1
is a distribution
\mathcal{D}
on
Y
given globally as the kernel of a
1
-form
\alpha
such that
\alpha\wedge(d\alpha)^{n-1}\neq 0
. The form
\alpha
is called a
contact form
. Whenever convenient we will refer to a pair
(Y,\alpha)
or to a pair
(Y,\mathcal{D})
as a contact manifold. The
Reeb vector field
is defined by the equations
\alpha(R_{\alpha})=1
and
d\alpha(R_{\alpha},\cdot)=0
.

A
Liouville domain

X_{0}
is an exact symplectic manifold with boundary whose Liouville vector field is transverse to the boundary and outward pointing (often referred to as
convexity
of the boundary); the restriction of
\lambda
to the boundary of
X_{0}
is a contact form
\alpha=\lambda|_{\partial X_{0}}
.

A
Liouville manifold
is an exact symplectic manifold which is cylindrical at infinity: there exists a Liouville domain
X_{0}\subset X
such that
X
can be identified with
X_{0}
attaching to an infinite cone:

X=X_{0}\cup_{\partial X_{0}}\left(\partial X_{0}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\right)

where
\left(\partial X_{0}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\right)
is equipped with the symplectic form
d(e^{s}\alpha)
; the symplectomorphism is induced by the flow of
Z
for time
s
. The infinite cone
\left(\partial X_{0}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},d(e^{s}\alpha)\right)
is called the
positive half symplectization
of the contact manifold
(\partial X_{0},\alpha)
, which we will denote by
S_{\geq 0}\partial X_{0}
. In general, for a contact manifold
(Y,\alpha)
where
\alpha>0
, one may construct a symplectic manifold
(SY,d(e^{s}\alpha))
in a similar manner by replacing
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
with
\mathbb{R}
, which we refer to as the
symplectization of
(\alpha,Y)
. The Liouville vector field in above local coordinates is
Z=\frac{\partial}{\partial s}
. We often use coordinate transformation
r=e^{s}
.

The image of
\partial X_{0}
under the Liouville flow is a family of contact manifolds parametrized by
s
, which are contactomorphic to each other. Thus there is a well-defined notion of “contact-type boundary at infinity”, which we denote it by
\partial_{\infty}X
. There is a natural embedding of the symplectization of
\partial_{\infty}X
into
X
. We assume
\partial_{\infty}X
is compact in general. However, the issues emanating from the non-compactness of
\partial_{\infty}X
on defining wrapped Fukaya category can be neutralized by taking a colimit of the compact cases over a homology hypercover (see
[
19
, Section 1.3]
).

An object invariant under the Liouville flow near infinity is called
cylindrical
; the infinite cone defined above is also called a
cylindrical end
.

Between Liouville manifolds, a
Liouville embedding
is an embedding
(X,\lambda)\xhookrightarrow{\phi}(X^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})
such that

(2.1)

\displaystyle\phi^{*}\lambda^{\prime}=\lambda+df\quad

\displaystyle\text{for some compactly supported $f$,}

\displaystyle\phi_{*}Z_{X}=Z_{X^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\text{outside a compact set.}

Here, the second condition is redundant if the codimension of
\phi
is zero.

Example 2.1
.

The Euclidean space
\mathbb{C}^{n}
with its standard symplectic structure
\omega_{\mathrm{std}}=\sum_{i}dx_{i}\wedge dy_{j}
is a Liouville manifold. The Liouville vector field in local coordinates is

\displaystyle Z=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i}\left(x_{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}+y_{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{i}}\right).

Example 2.2
.

The cotangent bundle
(T^{*}Q,\omega=-d\lambda_{\mathrm{can}})
of a compact manifold
Q
is a Liouville manifold, whose corresponding Liouville vector field
Z
is given by radial dilation in each fiber. Any tubular neighborhoods of the zero section with boundary transverse to
Z
can be chosen to be
X_{0}
. A canonical choice is to equip
Q
with a Riemannian metric, then choose
X_{0}
as the unit codisk bundle.

2.2.
Liouville actions

We now briefly recall the notions of symplectic and contact reductions.

Definition 2.3

(Symplectic reduction)
.

Let
(X,\omega)
be a symplectic manifold with a proper Hamiltonian action of a Lie group
G
, with the corresponding equivariant moment map
\mu:X\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{*}
such that

d\langle\mu,\xi\rangle=-\iota_{{X_{\xi}}}\omega

for
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}
. The symplectic reduction is

\displaystyle X/\!\!/G\coloneqq\mu^{-1}(0)/G.

If
G
acts freely on
\mu^{-1}(0)
(and hence
0
is a regular value of
\mu
), then
X/\!\!/G
is a smooth symplectic manifold.

Recall that a
contact action
is a group action preserving the contact structure
\mathcal{D}
. Regardless of whether the Lie group is compact, one can always find a contact form
\alpha
so that
\ker\alpha=\mathcal{D}
. This assertion follows by averaging the 1-form over some open covers induced by the contact action. For more details, see, for example,
[
41
]
.

Definition 2.4

(Contact reduction)
.

Let
(Y,\alpha)
be a contact manifold with a proper contact action of a Lie group
G
. The
contact moment map
is defined by

(2.2)

\mu:Y\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{*}:\langle\mu,\xi\rangle=\alpha(X_{\xi})

for
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}
. The contact reduction is defined by

\displaystyle Y/\!\!/G\coloneqq\mu^{-1}(0)/G.

Similarly to the symplectic case, if
G
acts freely on
\mu^{-1}(0)
and
0
is a regular value of the corresponding moment map
\mu
, the contact reduction is a smooth contact manifold with a contact form descended from
\alpha|_{\mu^{-1}(0)}
.

See
[
7
,
24
]
for more details (cf.
[
27
]
for a similar result but from a different perspective).

Let
(Y,\alpha)
be a contact manifold with a contact action of a Lie group
G
, with moment map
\mu_{Y}
. There is a natural way to extend it to a Hamiltonian action on its symplectization
SY
, by defining the moment map
\mu_{SY}:SY\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{*}
according to the formula:

(2.3)

\mu_{SY}(y,s)=e^{s}\mu_{Y}(y)

for all
(y,s)\in Y\times\mathbb{R}
. The corresponding action is given by

\displaystyle g\cdot(y,s)=(g\cdot y,s).

In particular, we have
\mathcal{L}_{Z}X_{\xi}=0
for all
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{*}
.

In order for the reduction of a Liouville manifold to remain a Liouville manifold, we introduce the following class of group actions.

Definition 2.5
.

Let
(X,\lambda)
be a Liouville manifold with a proper Hamiltonian group action by
G
with moment map
\mu_{X}
. We say this action is a
pre-Liouville action
if it satisfies the following two equivalent conditions:

(1)

Near infinity,
\mathcal{L}_{X_{\xi}}\lambda=0
and

(2.4)

\displaystyle\langle\mu_{X},\xi\rangle=\lambda(X_{\xi}).

(2)

There exists
\partial_{\infty}X\subset X
such that the restriction of
G
-action to
\partial_{\infty}X
is a contact action. The moment map
\mu_{X}
satisfies

(2.5)

\displaystyle\mu_{X}=e^{s}\mu_{\partial_{\infty}X}

on
S_{\geq 0}\partial_{\infty}X
, where
\mu_{\partial_{\infty}X}
is defined by Equation (
2.2
).

Here we view
\partial_{\infty}X
as a contact hypersurface near infinity. We refer to
\mu_{X}|_{\partial_{\infty}X}
as the restriction of
\mu_{X}
to
\partial_{\infty}X
, and refer to
\mu_{\partial_{\infty}X}
as the contact moment map defined by the induced contact action, which are actually equal by Equation (
2.4
).

Proof of equivalence in Definition
2.5
.

To show (1) implies (2), we observe that since
(\psi^{s}_{Z})^{*}\lambda=e^{s}\lambda
, the leaves of
G
-orbits lie in the level sets of
\psi^{s}_{Z}
. So WLOG, we may define
\partial_{\infty}X=\psi^{-1}(0)
. Then the restriction of
G
-action to
\partial_{\infty}X
is a contact action. Taking derivative of the following identity with respect to
Z
,

\displaystyle\langle\mu_{X},\xi\rangle=\lambda(X_{\xi}),

we have
Z\mu_{X}=\mu_{X}.
Combing the fact
\mu_{X}|_{\partial_{\infty}X}=\mu_{\partial_{\infty}X}
, we obtain the desired result.

Conversely, let
\alpha=\lambda|_{\partial_{\infty}X}
, then
\lambda=e^{s}\alpha
. Take derivative with respect to
Z
, we have
\mathcal{L}_{X_{\xi}}\lambda=0
. Equation (
2.4
) follows from combining
\lambda=e^{s}\alpha
and Equation (
2.5
).
∎

Definition 2.6
.

We say a proper group action
G
on
(X,\lambda)
is
a Liouville action
if it preserves the Liouville form
\lambda
.

The following proposition is well-established. For the sake of clarity, we present it here for future reference.

Proposition 2.7
.

A Liouville action is a pre-Liouville action.

Proof.

We define the moment map
\mu:X\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{*}
by:

(2.6)

\displaystyle\langle\mu(x),\xi\rangle=\lambda(X_{\xi})\text{ for all $x\in X$ and $\xi\in\mathfrak{g}$.}

One can check
\mu
is an equivariant map. We compute that

d\langle\mu,\xi\rangle=\mathcal{L}_{X_{\xi}}\lambda-\iota_{X_{\xi}}d\lambda=-\iota_{X_{\xi}}\omega

which shows it is a Hamiltonian action. It then follows from (1) of Definition
2.5
.
∎

Theorem 2.8
.

Let
(X,\lambda)
be a Liouville manifold with a pre-Liouville action by
G
. One can deform
\lambda
in a compact region so that the
G
-action on
(X,\overline{\lambda})
is a Liouville action. Moreover, we have
\overline{\lambda}=\lambda+df
where
f
is a compactly supported function.

Proof.

Since
G
is compact, one can find a bi-invariant volume form
dg
such that
\int_{G}dg=1
. Let
X_{0}
be a Liouville domain large enough such that the
G
-action preserves
\lambda|_{\partial X_{0}}
. We can average the primitive
1
-form in
X_{0}
by the following transformation:

\lambda\rightarrow\overline{\lambda}=\int_{G}\tau^{*}_{g}\lambda dg.

Since outside
\partial_{\infty}X
, the
1
-form is invariant under
G
-action, we have
\lambda=\overline{\lambda}
outside a compact subset. This implies that
(X_{0},\overline{\lambda})
is still a Liouville domain.

Since
\lambda
and
\overline{\lambda}
are in the same cohomology class, by our construction, we get

\overline{\lambda}-\lambda=df,

where
f
is a compactly supported function.
∎

Therefore, when discussing the wrapped Fukaya category of a Liouville manifold under a pre-Liouville action, the following homotopy argument shows that we can generally assume the group action preserves the Liouville form.

Corollary 2.9
.

In the same setting as Theorem
2.8
, there is a quasi-equivalence between the wrapped Fukaya categories
\mathcal{W}(X,\lambda)\cong\mathcal{W}(X,\overline{\lambda})
.

Proof.

By a Moser-type argument
[
13
, Proposition 11.8]
, there exists an isotopy
\phi_{t}:X\rightarrow X
of Liouville isomorphisms that intertwines
\lambda
and
\overline{\lambda}
. The wrapped Fukaya category is invariant under such deformations.
∎

Theorem 2.10
.

If a group
G
acts on a Liouville manifold
X
in a (pre)-Liouville fashion, and suppose
G
acts freely on
\mu^{-1}(0)
, then
X/\!\!/G
is a smooth Liouville manifold.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the symplectic case. Since
\lambda
vanishes in the direction of
G
-action by (
2.6
), it descend to a Liouville form on
X/\!\!/G
. To show
X/\!\!/G
is a Lioville manifold, we observe that for a contact manifold
(Y,\alpha)
, the symplectization commute with reduction, i.e.
(SY)/\!\!/G\cong S(Y/\!\!/G)
. If the action is only pre-Liouville, one can apply Theorem
2.8
.
∎

We will refer to
X/\!\!/G
as the
Liouville reduction of
X
.

Remark 2.11
.

The above theorem also shows that if we decompose
X
into
X_{0}\cup_{\partial X_{0}}\left(\partial X_{0}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\right)
, the symplectic reduction will preserve the decomposition. In the case of
G=\mathbb{S}^{1}
, this coincides with the notion of
contact cut
defined by
[
41
]
.

Example 2.12
.

(
\dim Q>\dim G
) Let
G
acts on a closed manifold
Q
,

\displaystyle G\rightarrow\mathrm{Diff}(X),\quad g\rightarrow\tau_{g}.

It lifts to a Hamiltonian action on
T^{*}Q
by

g\cdot(q,p)=(\tau_{g}q,(\tau_{g^{-1}})^{*}p).

The fact it is a Liouville action follows from the observation
\lambda_{\mathrm{can}}
is invariant under all automorphisms of
T^{*}Q
arising from the automorphisms of the base, then apply Proposition
2.7
.

The moment map of this action is

(2.7)

\displaystyle\mu(q,p)(\xi)=p(X_{\xi}(q)).

In the free case, the cotangent lifted action on
T^{*}X
is also free, hence
T^{*}X/\!\!/G
is a Liouville manifold. It turns out it is canonically symplectomorphic to
T^{*}(X/G)
. An explicit symplectomorphism can be constructed as follows: From Equation (
2.7
), one has

p\in\mu^{-1}(0)\cap T_{q}^{*}Q\Leftrightarrow p(X_{\xi}(q))=0.

In other words, the zero level set of
\mu
is the annihilator of the subbundle generated by the orbit of
G
. Now consider the map

\displaystyle\phi:TX/G

\displaystyle\rightarrow T(X/G)

\displaystyle\phi([v_{x}])

\displaystyle\rightarrow D_{x}\pi(v_{x})

where
D_{x}\pi
is the differential of the quotient map at
x
. It is easy to check that its dual is the desired symplectomorphism.

Theorem 2.13
.

Let
X
and
Y
be Liouville manifolds with Liouville
G
actions. Then the diagonal action of
G
on
X\times Y
is also Liouville.

Proof.

The product
X\times Y
is a Liouville manifold with Liouville form
\lambda_{X}+\lambda_{Y}
, which is invariant under the diagonal action.
∎

2.3.
Stopped Liouville manifolds and Liouville sectors

A
stopped Liouville manifold

(X,\mathfrak{f})
is Liouville manifold together with a closed subset
\mathfrak{f}\subset\partial_{\infty}X
called the
stop
.

This concept is closely related to the notion of a Liouville pair
(X,F)
where
F
is a
(2n-2)
-dimensional Liouville domain embedded in
\partial_{\infty}X
, called the
Liouville hypersurface
, as discussed in
[
64
,
19
]
. In this case, the stop is given as a closed neighborhood of
F
. We will often abuse the notation and identify the Liouville domain
F
with its completion.

It follows from Theorem
2.10
, if the stop is
G
-invariant, the symplectic reduction of a stopped a Liouville manifold is also a stopped Liouville manifold.

Lemma 2.14
.

Given a stopped Liouville manifold
(X,\mathfrak{f})
with a Liouville action by
G
, where
\mathfrak{f}
is
G
-invariant, the symplectic reduction of
(X,\mathfrak{f})
is a stopped Liouville manifold
(X/\!\!/G,\tilde{\mathfrak{f}})
, where
\tilde{\mathfrak{f}}
is the reduction of
\mathfrak{f}
.

In the following section, we will refine the above statement in the case of a Liouville pair.

The result of removing an open neighborhood of

F
is a Liouville manifold with boundary, called a
Liouville sector
. Near infinity, the Liouville sector can be identified with the symplectization of a contact manifold with a convex boundary.

Equivalently, a Liouville sector can be defined as a Liouville manifold with boundary, characterized by the existence of a
linear function
(i.e.
ZI=\alpha I
for some
\alpha>0
) along the boundary, as shown in
[
19
]
.

Definition 2.15
.

A Liouville sector is Liouville manifold with boundary satisfying the following condition:

•

For some (any)
\alpha>0
, there exists a function
I:\partial X\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with
ZI=\alpha I
near infinity and
X_{I}
is strictly outward pointing.

Without otherwise specified, we will always assume
\alpha=1
.

The existence of such a function carries rich geometric information around the boundary, leading to many important properties of the Liouville sector. For our purpose, we only state some of them:

(1)

One can deform the Liouville
1
-form by a compactly supported function so that the Liouville vector field
Z
is tangent to
\partial X
everywhere.

(2)

Every Liouville sector arises from a Liouville pair
(X,F)
.

(3)

The defining function
I
gives rise to a decomposition
\partial X=F\times\mathbb{R}
.

Therefore, we will implicitly identify a Liouville sector with its underlying Liouville pair and we shall always assume
Z
is tangent to its boundary.

In the spirit of Definition
2.5
, we define the following.

Definition 2.16
.

Let
X
be a Liouville sector. Let
(G,\mu)
be a group action together with a smooth equivariant function
\mu:X\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{*}.
We say
(G,\mu)
is a pre-Liouville action if

(1)

The restriction of
G
action to
X^{\circ}
is a Hamiltonian action with moment map
\mu|_{X^{\circ}}
;

(2)

Near infinity, it preserves
\lambda
and satisfies

(2.8)

\displaystyle\langle\mu_{X},\xi\rangle=\lambda(X_{\xi}).

Note that Equation (
2.8
) hold for both
G|_{\partial X}
and
G|_{X^{\circ}}
.

Definition 2.17
.

We say a proper group action
G
on a Liouville sector
(X,\lambda)
is
a Liouville action
if it preserves the Liouville form
\lambda
.

Theorem 2.18
.

Let
(X,\partial{X},\lambda)
be a Liouville sector with a pre-Liouville action by
G
. One can deform
\lambda
in a compact region so that the
G
-action on
(X,\overline{\lambda})
is a Liouville action. Moreover, we have
\overline{\lambda}=\lambda+df
where
f
is a compactly supported function.

Let
\iota:\partial X\rightarrow X
be the inclusion of the boundary into a symplectic manifold. Following conventions in
[
53
]
, we will refer to the restriction form

\omega_{\partial X}\coloneqq\iota^{*}\omega

as the
pre-symplectic form
. Its kernel defines a distribution whose integral curves give rise to an one-dimensional foliation
C
, called the
characteristic foliation
. For convenience, we also call the kernel characteristic foliation. The foliation is endowed with an orientation so that
\omega(N,C)>0
for any inward pointing vector
N
.
We first recall a Lemma proved by
[
53
]
.

Lemma 2.19

(Lemma 7.3 of
[
53
]
)
.

Fix a diffeomorphism

\phi:(X,\partial X)\to(X,\partial X)
and suppose
\phi^{*}\lambda=\lambda+df

for a function
f:X\to\mathbb{R}
, not necessarily compactly supported.
Then the restriction
\phi|_{\partial X}=\phi_{\partial}:\partial X\to\partial X

is a presymplectic diffeomorphism, i.e., satisfies
\phi_{\partial}^{*}\omega_{\partial X}=\omega_{\partial X}
.
In particular, it preserves the characteristic foliation of
\partial X
.

In particular, when
\phi
is given by a Liouville action, the above lemma tells us the characteristic foliation is preserved by the group action.

Theorem 2.20
.

There exists a defining function
ZI=I
such that
I
is a
G
-invariant function.

Proof.

Let
\hat{I}
be a prefixed
1
-defining function. Recall by our assumption,
\partial X
is completely contained in
S\partial_{\infty}X
, thus we can parametrize
\partial X
by the coordinate restricted from
S\partial_{\infty}X
. Since
G
is compact, we define

\displaystyle I=\int_{G}\hat{I}(g\cdot y,s)dg.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle ZI

\displaystyle=\int_{G}\frac{\partial}{\partial s}\hat{I}(g\cdot y,s)dg

\displaystyle=\int_{G}\hat{I}(g\cdot y,s)dg

\displaystyle=I.

By the Lemma
2.19
, the characteristic foliation is invariant under
G
, so for a characteristic foliation
C
:

\displaystyle dI|_{C}

\displaystyle=\int_{G}d\hat{I}(g\cdot y,s)|_{C}dg

\displaystyle=\int_{G}d\hat{I}(g\cdot y,s)|_{g\cdot C}dg

\displaystyle=\int_{G}\tau_{g}^{*}d\hat{I}(y,s)|_{C}dg>0

where the third equality follows from the fact symplectomorphism is orientation preserving.

∎

We now turn to a local description of Liouville action on a Liouville sector. The following theorem is an adaptation of the product decomposition near the boundary of a Liouville sector specified to the linear function
I
defined in Theorem
2.20
.

Corollary 2.21
.

Let
X
be a Liouville sector with a
G
-invariant defining function as given by Theorem
2.20
. Let
(X,F)
be the underlying Liouville pair. The symplectic reduction of
X
is again a Liouville sector, with its Liouville pair being
(X/\!\!/G,F/\!\!/G)
.

Proof.

By Theorem
2.10
,
X/\!\!/G
is a Liouville manifold with boundary. Since
\omega
and
\lambda
are
G
-invariant, the Liouville vector field
Z
is also
G
-invariant, which descends to
X/\!\!/G
. Applying Theorem
2.20
,
I
descends to a
1
-defining function for
X/\!\!/G
, which shows
X/\!\!/G
is a Liouville sector.

By
[
19
, Proposition 2.25]
, there is a decomposition near the boundary of
X
,

(2.9)

(X,\lambda_{X})=(F\times\mathbb{C}_{\mathrm{Re}\geq 0},\lambda_{F}+\lambda_{\mathbb{C}}+df),

in which
I=y
is the imaginary part of the
\mathbb{C}_{\mathrm{Re}\geq 0}
-coordinate,
\lambda_{\mathbb{C}}=-ydx
,
(F,\lambda_{F})
is a Liouville manifold and
f:F\times\mathbb{C}_{\mathrm{Re}\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
satisfies the following properties:

(1)

f
is supported inside
F_{0}\times\mathbb{C}
for some Liouville domain
F_{0}\subseteq F
.

(2)

f
coincides with some
f_{\pm\infty}:F\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
for
|I|
sufficiently large.

We remark that
f
is not a compactly supported function in general. By our choice of
I
, the leaves of
G|_{\partial X}
-orbits (i.e. restricted to
F\times\sqrt{-1}\mathbb{R}
) lie in
F\times\{y\}
for
y\in\sqrt{-1}\mathbb{R}
. Since
G
preserves the Liouville form and
f
is supported inside some Liouville domain, it follows that
G
preserves
\lambda_{F}
away from a compact set. In other words, this is a pre-Liouville action and
F/\!\!/G
is a Liouville manifold.
∎

With this understood, we study the lifted action on cotangent bundle of a manifold with boundary.

Example 2.22
.

(
\dim Q>\dim G\text{ and }\dim Q\geq 2
) Let
Q
be a connected
n
-manifold with boundary, and
G
a Lie group acting on
Q
. For simplicity, we assume
\partial Q
is connected. As shown in Example
2.12
, the cotangent lifted action in a Liouville action.
It follows that the symplectic reduction of the cotangent bundle is
T^{*}(Q/G)
, which is a Liouville sector with Liouville hypersurface being
T^{*}(\partial Q/G)
.

Example 2.23

(Open book decomposition)
.

Let
(M,\alpha)
be a contact manifold. An open book decomposition is a pair
(B,\pi)
where

(1)

B
is a codimension two submanifold called the
binding
of the open book and

(2)

\pi:M\setminus B\rightarrow\mathbb{S}^{1}
is a fibration of the complement of
B
such that each fiber
\pi^{-1}(\theta)
,
\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{1}
, corresponds to the interior of a compact hypersurface
\Sigma_{\theta}
, called the
pages
of the open book, with
\partial\Sigma_{\theta}
equal to
B
.

A page of the open book decomposition gives rise to a Liouville hypersurface as shown in
[
19
, Lemma 2.18]
. Therefore, suppose a Liouville action preserves the pages, we can apply Corollary
2.21
. The resulting symplectic reduction is a Liouville pair with its Liouville hypersurface being the reduction of the page.

Example 2.24

(Conic fibration)
.

Consider the conic fibration
\mathbb{C}^{2}\setminus\{\epsilon\}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\setminus\{\epsilon\}
given by
(x,y)\rightarrow xy
. Write polar coordinates
(x,y)=(r_{1}e^{i\theta_{1}},r_{2}e^{i\theta_{2}})
and consider the
\mathbb{S}^{1}
-action of weight
(1,-1)
. Then the Liouville form
r_{1}^{2}d\theta_{1}+r_{2}^{2}d\theta_{2}
is invariant under
\mathbb{S}^{1}
-action. We choose
F_{0}
as a page of the standard open book decomposition given by
\frac{xy}{|xy|}=-1
, in other words, they are the Liouville hypersurface specified by
\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}=\pi
.

The map
f:\mathbb{C}^{2}\setminus\{xy=\epsilon\}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\setminus\{\epsilon\}
can also be understood as the quotient map, so the stopped Liouville manifold downstairs is
(\mathbb{C}\setminus\{\epsilon\},-\infty)
.

3.
Homotopy quotient of Liouville manifolds

3.1.
Borel construction revisted

The classifying space
BG
of a compact Lie group
G
, together with its universal bundle
EG\rightarrow BG
, can be constructed via a sequence of finite-dimensional approximations. In particular, one may choose a collection of
N
-connected closed manifold equipped with free
G
-actions such that
EG=\bigcup_{N}EG(N)
(explicit constructions of these approximations will be introduced in the Section
3.3
).

(3.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G=EG(0)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces EG(1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces EG(N)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces EG}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pt=BG(0)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces BG(1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces BG(N)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces BG}

For each
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, the space
EG(N)
is an
N
-connected principal
G
-bundle over
BG(N).
The
G
-action induces a Liouville action on their cotangent bundles, and we have the canonical identification

\displaystyle T^{*}EG(N)/\!\!/G=T^{*}(EG(N)/G)=T^{*}BG(N).

We choose a family of
G
-invariant Riemannian metrics
\{g_{N}\}
on
EG(N)
such that the restriction of each metric agrees with the one on the preceding stratum in the approximation tower.

In general, for an embedded submanifold
i:X\hookrightarrow Y
, there is a natural exact sequence of vector bundles:

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow N^{*}X\longrightarrow i^{*}T^{*}Y\longrightarrow T^{*}X\longrightarrow 0.

A choice of Riemannian metric determines a splitting of this sequence, and hence an embedding of cotangent bundles as in
(
3.2
). In the same manner, the cotangent bundle
T^{*}EG
is approximated by the sequence
T^{*}EG(N)
.

For each cotangent bundle
T^{*}EG(N)
, the
G
-bundle
EG(N)
embeds as the zero section
0_{EG(N)}\hookrightarrow T^{*}EG(N)
, which is a
G
-invariant exact Lagrangian contained in
\mu^{-1}(0)
. Its reduction is
0_{BG(N)}\subset T^{*}BG(N)
. We note that
0_{EG(N)}
and
0_{BG(N)}
are compatible with the embeddings in (
3.2
).

(3.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{*}G=T^{*}EG(0)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{*}EG(1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{*}EG(N)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{*}EG}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pt=T^{*}BG(0)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{*}BG(1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{*}BG(N)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{*}BG}

Let
(X,\lambda)
be a Liouville manifold (or sector) and let
G\rightarrow\mathrm{Aut}(X,\lambda)
be a Liouville action. We denote by
\mu_{X}
the moment map for the action of
G
on
X
, and by
\mu_{T^{*}EG(N)}
the moment map for the Liouville action of
G
on
T^{*}EG(N)
, obtained by lifting the
G
-action on
EG(N)
.

The diagonal action of
G
on
(X\times T^{*}EG(N),\lambda\oplus\lambda_{T^{*}EG(N)})
is a free Liouville action. Here
\lambda_{T^{*}EG(N)}
denotes the canonical one form of
T^{*}EG(N)
.

To define the model of the Borel construction that we will use, we give two equivalent descriptions. The first is the associated bundle of the universal
G
-bundle, that is the classical Borel construction. The second one is obtained by the Liouville reduction of the diagonal action above. These two constructions agree by an argument analogous to the classical
Sternberg–Weinstein universal phase space construction

[
66
]
.

Definition 3.1
.

Let
X
be a Liouville manifold (or sector) equipped with a Liouville action of
G
. For each
N
, the
Borel–Liouville space

X(N)
is defined by either of the following equivalent constructions (up to choices of Riemannian metrics) :

•

(
Construction I
) The associated bundle of the
G
-principal bundle
\mu_{T^{*}EG(N)}^{-1}(0)\rightarrow T^{*}BG(N)
, i.e.
X(N)\coloneqq X\times_{G}\mu_{T^{*}EG(N)}^{-1}(0)
.

•

(
Construction II
) The Liouville reduction
X(N)\coloneqq\left(X\times T^{*}EG(N)\right)/\!\!/G=\left(\mu_{X}+\mu_{T^{*}EG(N)}\right)^{-1}(0)/G
.

We define the
Borel–Liouville construction
(or simply Borel construction) to be

\displaystyle X_{G}\coloneqq\varinjlim_{N}X(N).

By Construction I,
\mu_{T^{*}EG}^{-1}(0)
deformation retracts onto its base
EG
in a
G
-equivariant way, and hence has the same homotopy type as
EG
. it follows that
X_{G}
is homotopy equivalent to the classical Borel construction.

By Construction I, there is a fibration

(3.3)

To prove the equivalence of two constructions, we begin by analyzing how the above fibration structure is realized using Construction II. A symplectic analogue appears in
[
11
]
.

Fix
q\in BG(N)
, let
O_{q}\coloneqq T_{q}(G\cdot q)
denote the tangent space to the
G
-orbit passing through
q
. As explained in Example
2.12
, the zero level set of the moment map
\mu_{T^{*}EG(N)}
is the annihilator of the subbundle generated by the
G
-orbits.
We choose a
G
-invariant metric on
EG(N)
, which determines an orthogonal splitting

\displaystyle T_{q}EG(N)=O_{q}\oplus O_{q}^{\perp}\cong\mathfrak{g}\oplus O_{q}^{\perp}.

Passing to duals, we obtain a corresponding spiltting
T_{q}^{*}EG(N)=\mathfrak{g}^{*}\oplus(O_{q}^{\perp})^{*}
.
We set
(\mathcal{O}^{\perp})^{*}=\bigcup_{q}(O_{q}^{\perp})^{*}
, so that

\mu_{T^{*}EG(N)}^{-1}(0)=(\mathcal{O}^{\perp})^{*}.

Thus, the orthogonal projection
T^{*}EG(N)\rightarrow(\mathcal{O}^{\perp})^{*}
,
composed with the quotient projection induces a projection
T^{*}EG(N)\rightarrow T^{*}BG(N)
. Restricting to the zero level set of the diagonal moment map gives a
G
-invariant map

(3.4)

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathrm{diag}}^{-1}(0)\hookrightarrow X\times T^{*}EG(N)\rightarrow T^{*}BG(N)

which descends to a projection
X(N)\xrightarrow{\pi_{N}}T^{*}BG(N)
. We now show that the fiber of
\pi_{N}
is diffeomorphic to
X
. For any
b=(q_{b},p_{b})\in T^{*}BG(N)
, the inclusion
i_{b}
of the fiber
\pi^{-1}_{N}(b)
is given explicitly by

(3.5)

i_{b}:X\rightarrow X(N):x\mapsto\left[x,(q_{b},p_{b}-\mu_{X}(x))\right]

where we identify
\mu_{X}(x)\in\mathfrak{g}^{*}\cong O_{q}^{*}
.

Proof of equivalence in Definition
3.1
.

Let

\displaystyle X(N)\coloneqq\left(X\times T^{*}EG(N)\right)/\!\!/G,\quad X(N)^{\prime}\coloneqq X\times_{G}\mu^{-1}_{T^{*}EG(N)}(0).

G
-bundles between these two models. Consider the diagram

where the vertical map
\Phi
is defined on representatives by

\Phi([x,(q,p)])=[x,(q,\operatorname{pr}_{(\mathcal{O}^{\perp})^{*}}(p))].

Here,
\operatorname{pr}_{(\mathcal{O}^{\perp})^{*}}
denotes the orthogonal projection onto the annihilator of the orbit directions.

It is straightforward to check
\Phi
is a
G
-invariant isomorphism.
∎

From Construction II, the Liouville reduction endows
X(N)
with a Liouville
1
-form
\lambda_{X(N)}
. We now show that this Liouville structure is compatible with the fibration.

Remark 3.2
.

The two-form
(\Phi^{-1})^{*}d\lambda_{X(N)}
on
X(N)^{\prime}
defines a weak coupling form and hence determines a Hamiltonian connection. In particular, this form coincides with the symplectic structure on the symplectic Borel space used in
[
33
]
.

Lemma 3.3
.

The fibration (
3.3
) is compatible with the Liouville form
\lambda_{X(N)}
and the symplectic form
\omega_{X(N)}
obtained in Theorem
2.10
, in the sense that

(3.7)

\displaystyle i_{b}^{*}\lambda_{X(N)}=\lambda_{X},\quad i_{b}^{*}\omega_{X(N)}=\omega_{X}

where
i_{b}
denotes the inclusion of the fiber at a point
b\in T^{*}BG(N)
.

Proof.

Let
x\in X
and
v\in T_{x}X
. From the explicit formula for
i_{b}
in (
3.5
), we have

\displaystyle\left((i_{b})^{*}\lambda_{X(N)}\right)_{x}(v)=\lambda_{X}(v)_{x}+\lambda_{\mathrm{can}}(-(d\mu_{X})_{x}(v)).

By the property of the canonical
1
-form,
\lambda_{\mathrm{can}}(-(d\mu_{X})_{x}(v))
vanishes since
-(d\mu_{X})_{x}(v)
lies in the vertical direction. Taking derivatives establishes the compatibility of symplectic forms as well.
∎

Definition
3.1
shows that whenever a Liouville manifold carries a Liouville
G
-action, one may construct a model of Borel construction obtained by finite-dimensional approximations of Liouville manifolds.
1
1

1

Throughout this paper we do not address whether the direct limit
X_{G}
carries a Liouville structure. Our use of the Borel construction relies only on the finite-stage Liouville manifold.

Recall that a connection on a fiber bundle
\pi:E\rightarrow B
is a splitting of the tangent bundle

(3.8)

TE\cong TE^{\operatorname{hor}}\oplus TE^{\operatorname{vert}},

where
TE^{\operatorname{vert}}=\ker d\pi
. As usual, we refer to
TE^{\operatorname{vert}}
(resp.
TE^{\operatorname{hor}}
) as the
vertical (resp. horizontal) bundle
.

In particular, the symplectic form
\omega_{X(N)}
determines a canonical connection on the fibration

\displaystyle\pi_{N}:X(N)\rightarrow T^{*}BG(N),

given by the
\omega_{X(N)}
-orthogonal complement of
TX(N)^{\operatorname{vert}}
. We refer to this as the
connection associated with
\omega_{X(N)}
. Let us conclude this section by recording some standard properties of this connection.

Remark 3.4

(Parallel transport)
.

The connection determines a well-defined notion of parallel transport, which allows us to construct local trivializations via families of parallel transports.

The differential of the projection

d\pi_{N}:T_{x}X(N)\rightarrow T_{\pi_{N}(x)}T^{*}BG(N)

is surjective everywhere and restricts to an isomorphism

d\pi_{N}|_{\mathrm{hor}}:T_{x}X(N)^{\operatorname{hor}}\rightarrow T_{\pi_{N}(x)}T^{*}BG(N).

Given a vector
Y\in T_{\pi_{N}(x)}T^{*}BG(N)
, we denote by
Y^{\#}
its
horizontal lift
, namely the preimage of
Y
under
d\pi_{N}|_{\mathrm{hor}}
.

For any path
\gamma:[0,1]\rightarrow T^{*}BG(N)
, the associated parallel transport

\displaystyle\Phi_{[\gamma]}:F_{\gamma(0)}\rightarrow F_{\gamma(1)}

is a symplectomorphism between the fibers
(F_{\gamma(0)},\omega_{\gamma(0)})
and
(F_{\gamma(1)},\omega_{\gamma(1)})
.

3.2.
Borel construction of Lagrangians

Let
(X,\omega,\mu)
be a symplectic manifold with a Hamiltonian
G
-action. If the
G
-action is free, then every
G
-invariant Lagrangian
L\subset\mu^{-1}(0)
corresponds uniquely to a Lagrangian

\displaystyle\underline{L}\coloneqq L/G.

Thus the map
L\rightarrow\underline{L}
defines a one-to-one correspondence between
G
-invariant Lagrangians
L
in the zero level set and Lagrangians in
X/\!\!/G
.

The moment map
\mu
is constant on any connected
G
-invariant Lagrangian
L
(see, for example,
[
14
, Lemma 4.1]
); we denote this constant by
\mu(L)
. Hence in Construction II, the reduction of the Lagrangian

L\times(b,-\mu(L))\subset\mu_{\mathrm{diag}}^{-1}(0)

defines a Lagrangian submanifold of
X(N)
, lying over the zero section of
T^{*}BG(N)
. We denote these Lagrangians by
L(N)
. These Lagrangians correspond bijectively to Lagrangians
L\times_{G}0_{EG(N)}
in Construction I by the identification established earlier. This fits into the diagram

(3.9)

We set

\displaystyle L_{G}\coloneqq\varinjlim_{N}L(N),

which recovers the usual Borel construction of
L
. Since each
L(N)
is Lagrangian, parallel transport along the paths contained in
0_{BG(N)}
, computed using connection associated with
\omega_{X(N)}
, preserves the Lagrangian fiber
L
.

The preceding discussion applies to general symplectic manifolds and Lagrangians. In the exact setting, we additionally require the primitives of exact Lagrangians be chosen
G
-invariant. This can always be achieved. Indeed, let
f_{L}:L\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a primitive of
L
, we may replace
f_{L}
by its averaged function over
G
which still satisfies
df_{L}=\lambda|_{L}
as
\lambda
is
G
-invariant. This function also serves as a primitive for the reduced Lagrangian
\underline{L}
.
