.

Remark 7.3
.

The rest of the discussion still holds if we allow the fibers of
L\cap N
over
L^{\prime}
to be
\mathbb{D}_{\Re}\cap\mathbb{D}_{\Im}
, i.e., a union of two thimbles. In this case,
L
is immersed.

Here,
S^{1}
is a Legenderian circle lying inside
S^{3}\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}_{z,w}
. Thus

is cylindrical near infinity in

. A similar argument shows that
\underline{L}^{\circ}
is a cylindrical Lagrangian of

.
∎

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
L_{i}\subset\mu^{-1}(0)
be exact,
\mathbb{S}^{1}
-invariant, immersed Lagrangians which are of disk type near
F
. Then there is an isomorphism

HW_{\mathbb{S}^{1}}^{\bullet}(L^{\circ}_{1},L^{\circ}_{2})\cong HW^{\bullet}(\underline{L}^{\circ}_{1},\underline{L}^{\circ}_{2}).

Proof.

The
\mathbb{S}^{1}
-action on
L^{\circ}\subset X^{\circ}
is free. The result follows from Theorem
6.15
.
∎

To relate
HF_{\mathbb{S}^{1}}^{\bullet}(L_{1},L_{2})
to
HW_{\mathbb{S}^{1}}^{\bullet}(L^{\circ}_{1},L^{\circ}_{2})
, we extend the Hamiltonians defined in (
7.5
) in a similar manner. Let
N
denote a neighborhood of
F
which is symplectomorphic to a disc bundle
DN_{F/X}
, which can be viewed as a union of local models (
7.2
). We choose Hamiltonians
H_{\nu}
on
X
satisfying:

(1)

H_{\nu}
is
C^{2}
-small on the complement
X\setminus N
, and

(2)

on a small model neighborhood
U\subset N
appearing in (
7.2
),
H_{\nu}
is of product form

H_{\nu}|_{U}=K_{\nu}\times H^{\prime}|_{V},

where
K_{\nu}
is the Hamiltonian on
\mathbb{C}^{2}
defined in (
7.5
) and
H^{\prime}
is a
C^{2}
-small Hamiltonian on
F
chosen so that intersections
L^{\prime}_{1}\cap\phi_{H^{\prime}}^{1}(L_{2}^{\prime})
are transverse.

The intersection
L_{1}\cap\phi_{H_{\nu}}^{1}(L_{2})
decomposes into two types of components: intersections in
X\setminus N
and those in
N
. We can further decompose the latter into two parts, as in the local
\mathbb{C}^{2}
model. For each transverse intersection
L_{1}^{\prime}\cap\phi_{H^{\prime}}^{1}(L_{2}^{\prime})
, we obtain
\nu
cleanly intersecting free
\mathbb{S}^{1}
-orbits with a single
\mathbb{S}^{1}
-fixed point. Thus, we can write

\displaystyle CF^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{S}^{1}}(L^{\circ}_{1},L^{\circ}_{2};H_{\nu}^{\circ})

\displaystyle=CF^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{S}^{1}}(L_{1},L_{2};H_{\nu})_{X\setminus N}\oplus CF^{\bullet}(L_{1}^{\prime},L_{2}^{\prime};H^{\prime})\otimes\mathbb{C}[\lambda,t]\langle 1,x,\ldots x^{\nu}\rangle,

\displaystyle CF^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{S}^{1}}(L_{1},L_{2};H_{\nu})

\displaystyle=CF^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{S}^{1}}(L_{1},L_{2};H_{\nu})_{X\setminus N}\oplus CF^{\bullet}(L_{1}^{\prime},L_{2}^{\prime};H^{\prime})\otimes\left(\mathbb{C}[\lambda,t]\langle 1,x,\ldots x^{\nu}\rangle\oplus\mathbb{C}[\lambda]\langle p^{\nu+1}\rangle\right).

Here,
CF^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{S}^{1}}(L_{1},L_{2};H_{\nu})_{X\setminus N}
denotes the intersections appearing in
X\setminus N
. As a generalization of (
7.8
), we obtain the following.

Proposition 7.6
.

Let
L_{i}\subset\mu^{-1}(0)
be exact,
\mathbb{S}^{1}
-invariant Lagrangians which are of disk type near
F
. Then the inclusion
i:CF^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{S}^{1}}(L^{\circ}_{1},L^{\circ}_{2};H_{\nu}^{\circ})\to CF^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{S}^{1}}(L_{1},L_{2};H_{\nu})
induces an isomorphism

\lim_{\nu\to\infty}HF_{\mathbb{S}^{1}}^{\bullet}(L^{\circ}_{1},L^{\circ}_{2};H^{\circ}_{\nu})\cong HF_{\mathbb{S}^{1}}^{\bullet}(L_{1},L_{2}).

Proof.

We first claim that the argument holds when
X=N
. In this case, the Floer complexes can be written as tensor products:.

(7.10)

\begin{split}CF^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{S}^{1}}(L^{\circ}_{1},L^{\circ}_{2};H_{\nu}^{\circ})&=CF^{\bullet}(L_{1}^{\prime},L_{2}^{\prime};H^{\prime})\otimes\mathbb{C}[\lambda,t]\langle 1,x,\ldots x^{\nu}\rangle,\\
CF^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{S}^{1}}(L_{1},L_{2};H_{\nu})&=CF^{\bullet}(L_{1}^{\prime},L_{2}^{\prime};H^{\prime})\otimes\left(\mathbb{C}[\lambda,t]\langle 1,x,\ldots x^{\nu}\rangle\oplus\mathbb{C}[\lambda]\langle p^{\nu+1}\rangle\right).\end{split}

We may choose almost complex structures
J_{N}
on
N
and
J_{F}
on
F
that are compatible with the bundle projection
N\to F
, by choosing a Hermitian connection on
N
and requiring that
J_{N}
preserves the horizontal and vertical subspaces. For any pseudo-holomorphic strip in
N
from
a_{1}\otimes b_{1}
to
a_{2}\otimes b_{2}
, its projection to
F
is a strip from
a_{1}
to
a_{2}
. This implies that the differentials of Floer complexes in (
7.10
) respects the action filtration of
CF^{\bullet}(L_{1}^{\prime},L_{2}^{\prime};H^{\prime})
.

For
N^{\circ}
, as Hamiltonian chords wrap faster around the zero section
F
, their action values become increasingly negative. This implies that strips between non-trivial Hamiltonian chords preserves or decreases the wrapping number. Therefore, the only possible difference between the differentials in (
7.10
) arises from strips connecting non-trivial Hamiltonian chords to
\mathbb{S}^{1}
-fixed points, i.e., the generator of the form
\psi\otimes\lambda^{i}p^{\nu+1}
for some
i
and
\nu
.

Consider the image of a generator
\psi\otimes\lambda^{i}p^{\nu+1}
under the continuation map
c_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
from
H_{\nu}
to
H_{\nu^{\prime}}
. Since the action of
CF^{\bullet}(L_{1}^{\prime},L_{2}^{\prime};H^{\prime})
is bounded, for sufficiently large
\nu^{\prime}>\nu\gg 0
, the pseudo-holomorphic strips governing
c_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
are confined in the fiber over a point
p\in L_{1}^{\prime}\cap\phi_{H^{\prime}}^{1}(L_{2}^{\prime})
. By the same reasoning as in (
7.8
), we conclude that the continuation image of
\psi\otimes\lambda^{i}p^{\nu+1}
contains no
\mathbb{S}^{1}
-fixed points terms
\phi\otimes\lambda^{i}p^{\nu+1}
and instead becomes a linear combination of
\psi^{\prime}\otimes\lambda^{i^{\prime}}x^{\nu^{\prime}}
for
\nu^{\prime}\gg 0
. This implies that any strip connecting a non-constant Hamiltonian chords to
\psi\otimes\lambda^{i}p^{\nu}
is absorbed after passing to the colimit
\nu\to\infty
, and does not contribute. This finishes the proof in the case
X=N
.

Second, we must show the result remains valid after gluing
N
with
X\setminus N
. For
N^{\circ}
, as Hamiltonian orbits wrap faster around the boundary, their action values become more negative. This implies that there are no strips from
X^{\circ}\setminus N^{\circ}
to nonconstant Hamiltonian chords inside
N^{\circ}
. Again, the difference between
CF^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{S}^{1}}(L^{\circ}_{1},L^{\circ}_{2};H_{\nu}^{\circ})
and
CF^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{S}^{1}}(L_{1},L_{2};H_{\nu})
is contributed by strips from the generators of
CF^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{S}^{1}}(L_{1},L_{2};H_{\nu})_{X\setminus N}
to the generators
\psi\otimes\lambda^{i}p^{\nu+1}\in CF^{\bullet}(L_{1}^{\prime},L_{2}^{\prime};H^{\prime})\otimes\mathbb{C}[\lambda]\cdot p^{\nu+1}
.

The rest of the arguments proceeds exactly as in the case of
X=N
. Under the continuation map
c_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
from
H_{\nu}
to
H_{\nu^{\prime}}
, the generator
\psi\otimes\lambda^{i}p^{\nu+1}
is mapped to a linear combination of
\psi^{\prime}\otimes\lambda^{i^{\prime}}x^{\nu^{\prime}}
for
\nu^{\prime}\gg 0
. This implies that any such contribution from
CF^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{S}^{1}}(L_{1},L_{2};H_{\nu})_{X\setminus N}
to
CF^{\bullet}(L_{1}^{\prime},L_{2}^{\prime};H^{\prime})\otimes\mathbb{C}[\lambda]\langle p^{\nu+1}\rangle
is also absorbed after passing to the colimit
\nu\to\infty
, and does not survive in cohomology. This proves the claim.

∎

Considering a colimit over positive isotopies along
\partial X
, we obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 7.7
.

Let
L_{i}\subset\mu^{-1}(0)
be exact,
\mathbb{S}^{1}
-invariant Lagrangians which are of disk type near
F
. Then we have

HW_{\mathbb{S}^{1}}^{\bullet}(L_{1},L_{2})\cong HW^{\bullet}(\underline{L}^{\circ}_{1},\underline{L}^{\circ}_{2}).

The same proof of Proposition
7.6
applies when the singularities of a given
\mathbb{S}^{1}
-action is nodal. Therefore, we have the following.

Theorem 7.8
.

Let
\mathbb{S}^{1}\circlearrowright(X,\lambda)
be a Liouville domain with a Hamiltonian
\mathbb{S}^{1}
-action satisfying the following assumptions:

(1)

The isotropy group for each
p\in\mu^{-1}(0)
is either trivial or the whole
\mathbb{S}^{1}
. We denote the fixed locus by
F=(\mu^{-1}(0))^{\mathbb{S}^{1}}
.

(2)

Each connected component of
F
is a codimension-four submanifold, admitting an equivariant symplectic neighborhood
U\subset X
of the form (
7.2
), that is, modeled on a conic fibration.

Then
X\setminus F
can be equipped with an
\mathbb{S}^{1}
-equivariant convex Liouville structure near the puncture
F
, which descends to the regular locus of its quotient

\underline{X}^{\circ}\coloneqq(\mu^{-1}\{0\}-F)/\mathbb{S}^{1}.

For any
\mathbb{S}^{1}
-invariant Lagrangians
L_{i}\subset\mu^{-1}(0)
which are of disk type near
F
, we have

HW^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{S}^{1}}(L_{1},L_{2})\simeq HW^{\bullet}(\underline{L}_{1}^{\circ},\underline{L}_{2}^{\circ}),

where
\underline{L}^{\circ}\coloneqq(L-F)/\mathbb{S}^{1}
.

7.3.
Iterated conic fibration

In this subsection, we apply Corollary
7.7
inductively to extend our results to an iterated conic fibration. Let
\underline{X}
be a fixed smooth quasi-projective variety equipped with sections
\{s_{i}\in\Gamma(Y,\mathcal{L}_{i}^{\otimes 2})\}_{i=1}^{n}
of ample line bundles. We assume that
\{\underline{D}_{i}=Z(s_{i}=0)\subset Y\}_{i=1}^{n}
form a simple normal crossing divisor with each
\underline{D}_{i}
being smooth. In this setup we iterate conic fibration construction to obtain:

\displaystyle X_{k}\coloneqq Z(z_{i}w_{i}=s_{i})

\displaystyle\subset\mathrm{Tot}_{Y}(\oplus_{i=1}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{i}^{\oplus 2}),

\displaystyle X\coloneqq X_{n}\xrightarrow{\pi_{n}}X_{n-1}\xrightarrow{\pi_{n-1}}

\displaystyle\cdots\xrightarrow{\pi_{2}}X_{1}\xrightarrow{\pi_{1}}X_{0}\eqqcolon\underline{X},

where

(7.11)

\displaystyle\pi_{k}(\mathbf{x},z_{1},w_{i},\ldots,z_{k-1},w_{k-1},z_{k},w_{k})

\displaystyle\coloneqq(\mathbf{x},z_{1},w_{i},\ldots,z_{k-1},w_{k-1}).

The total space
X
is equipped with a Hamiltonian torus action

(\mathbb{S}^{1})^{n}\circlearrowright X,\quad(\lambda_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\cdot(\mathbf{x},w_{i},z_{i})_{i=1}^{n}=(\mathbf{x},\lambda_{i}w_{i},\lambda_{i}^{-1}z_{i})_{i=1}^{n}.

Let us denote the circle action acting on the
k
-th coordinate by
\mathbb{S}^{1}_{k}
. Then each
X_{k-1}
is a singular reduction
X_{k}\sslash\mathbb{S}^{1}_{k}
with degeneracy locus
\pi_{k}^{-1}\circ\cdots\pi_{1}^{-1}(\underline{D}_{k})
. Define

X_{k}^{\circ}\coloneqq X_{k}-\bigcup_{i=k+1}^{n}D_{k,i},\quad D_{k,i}\coloneqq\pi_{k}^{-1}\circ\cdots\pi_{1}^{-1}(\underline{D}_{i}).

The reduction
X_{k}^{\circ}\sslash\mathbb{S}^{1}_{k}
is singular along
D_{k,k}
, and its non-singular locus is
X^{\circ}_{k}
.

To apply previous results, we impose similar assumptions on
\underline{X}
as before; we assume that there exists a smooth projective compactification
\underline{Y}\supset\underline{X}
of
\underline{X}
by a SNC divisor
\underline{K}
, and that each
\underline{D}_{i}
extends to a smooth ample divisor of
\underline{Y}
. Since
X_{k}^{\circ}
is obtained as a divisor complement, the same holds for
X_{k-1}^{\circ}
. Both spaces are equipped with the Liouville structure described in (
7.1
). We choose Lagrangians compatibly with this structure: we say that an
(\mathbb{S}^{1})^{n}
-invariant Lagrangian is of disk type if it is of disk type near each
D_{j,n}
for each
j
. We then inductively define

\underline{L}_{n}^{\circ}\coloneqq L\cap X^{\circ}_{n},\quad\underline{L}_{k}^{\circ}\coloneqq L_{k+1}/(\mathbb{S}^{1})_{k+1}\cap X^{\circ}_{k}.

By inductively applying Proposition
7.6
, we have the following result.

Theorem 7.9
.

Let
L_{i}\subset\mu^{-1}(0)
be exact,
(\mathbb{S}^{1})^{n}
-invariant Lagrangians that are of disk type near the singularities. Then, for each
k=1,\cdots,n
, there is an isomorphism

HF^{\bullet}_{(\mathbb{S}^{1})^{n}}(L_{1},L_{2})\simeq HF_{(\mathbb{S}^{1})^{n-k}}^{\bullet}(\underline{L}^{\circ}_{1,k},\underline{L}^{\circ}_{2,k}),

which is a cohomology version of
[
40
, Conjecture A]
.
